<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every holomorphic one-parameter family of isolated hypersurface singularities in ℂ<sup>3</sup> with constant Milnor number is topologically right-trivial. This gives a positive answer to the surface case of the <i>μ</i>-constant problem. The trivialization fixes the parameter and the origin section, and preserves the defining functions.

- **claim-main-right-trivial** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $F:(\mathbb C^3\times\Delta,\{0\}\times\Delta)\to(\mathbb C,0)$ be holomorphic. Suppose that, for every $t\in\Delta$, $f_t(0)=0$, $f_t$ has an isolated critical point at $0$, and $\mu(f_t,0)=\mu(f_0,0)$. After shrinking the disk and the germ representatives, there is a homeomorphism germ $$\Phi(x,t)=(\phi_t(x),t),\qquad \phi_t(0)=0,\quad \phi_0=\mathrm{id},\qquad f_t\bigl(\phi_t(x)\bigr)=f_0(x).$$ Both $\Phi$ and $\Phi^{-1}$ are jointly continuous on neighborhoods of the origin section over the original disk.
  - Explanation: A holomorphic family of isolated hypersurface singularities in three variables with constant Milnor number is, after shrinking, topologically right-trivial. The trivialization fixes the parameter and the origin section, preserves the defining function, and it and its inverse are jointly continuous.
- **claim-local-family-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-family-properties)): There are representatives on which the spatial critical locus of $F$ is exactly $\Gamma$. On these representatives $X\to\Delta$ is flat, its fibers are normal Gorenstein surfaces, and $X$ is a normal Gorenstein threefold, smooth over $\Delta$ away from $\Gamma$. The same statements hold after a nonconstant holomorphic base change between smooth disks.
  - Explanation: On suitable representatives the spatial critical locus is exactly the origin section. The zero set is then flat over the disk with normal Gorenstein fibers and is itself a normal Gorenstein threefold, and this persists after a disk base change.
- **claim-fixed-ball-euler** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-ball-euler)): There are a sufficiently small closed Euclidean ball $B$ about $0$, a smaller parameter disk, and $\eta>0$ such that the following statements hold for every parameter in that disk:* 1. *$0$ is the only spatial critical point in $B$;* 2. *$f_t^{-1}(c)$ meets $\partial B$ transversely whenever $\|c\|<\eta$;* 3. *$$\chi(X_t\cap B)=1, \qquad \chi_c\bigl((X_t\cap B)\setminus\{0\}\bigr)=0.$$* *The Euler equalities also hold if the radius of $B$ varies slightly within a sufficiently small interval about the chosen radius.
  - Explanation: A single small closed ball works for all parameters in a smaller disk. The origin is the only spatial critical point there, small levels meet the sphere transversely, and the zero fiber in the ball has the Euler characteristic of a point while the punctured fiber has compactly supported Euler characteristic zero.
- **claim-individual-jets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-individual-jets)): Let $f\in\mathbb C\{x_1,x_2,x_3\}$ have an isolated critical point at $0$ and Milnor number $\mu$, and write $\mathfrak m=(x_1,x_2,x_3)$. For every $b\in\mathfrak m^{\mu+2}$ there is a biholomorphic germ $h$ fixing $0$ such that $(f+b)\circ h=f$. In particular, if $N\geq\mu+1$, then $f$ is analytically right-equivalent to its Taylor polynomial $j^Nf$.
  - Explanation: Adding any term of sufficiently high order to an isolated singularity does not change its analytic right type. Hence the germ is right-equivalent to a Taylor polynomial of degree at least the Milnor number plus one.
- **claim-projective-local-square** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-projective-local-square)): Let $V$ be an integral normal projective complex surface, smooth outside a point $p$, with Cartier canonical divisor. Let $b:S\to V$ be a projective birational morphism from a normal surface, isomorphic outside $p$. Suppose that the reduced inverse image of $p$ is a divisor $B$, that $(S,B)$ is log canonical, and that $K_S+B$ is $b$-nef. Then $$\label{eq:projective-local-square} \beta(V,p)=K_V^2-(K_S+B)^2.$$
  - Explanation: Wahl's local invariant of a surface point is computed on a projective model as the difference between the canonical square of the surface and the square of the log canonical divisor of a nef log canonical partial resolution.
- **claim-lc-persistence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lc-persistence)): In a holomorphic family of isolated hypersurface singularities in three spatial variables with constant Milnor number over a connected smooth complex curve, log canonicity of the normal surface germ is constant.
  - Explanation: Constancy of the spectrum in a constant-Milnor-number family, together with the threshold formula and inversion of adjunction, makes log canonicity of the surface germ constant along the family.
- **claim-mu-jet-locus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mu-jet-locus)): The subset $U_\mu$ is locally closed algebraic. On $U_\mu$, the function $g\mapsto\beta(V(g),0)$ has finite image and is algebraically constructible.
  - Explanation: The jets with prescribed Milnor number form a locally closed algebraic set on which Wahl's invariant takes finitely many values and is constructible.
- **claim-scalar-curve-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar-curve-reduction)): Assume the following algebraic assertion: for every polynomial family in three variables with regular coefficients on a smooth complex algebraic curve $C$, with zero constant and linear terms and constant positive Milnor number at the origin, the Wahl invariant at a marked non-log-canonical fiber equals its value on the nearby general fibers. Then $\beta(X_t,0)$ is constant near $t=0$ in every holomorphic $\mu$-constant family whose central surface germ is non-log-canonical.
  - Explanation: Granting constancy of the invariant for polynomial families over algebraic curves, a curve-selection argument in the jet locus gives constancy in every holomorphic constant-Milnor-number family with non-log-canonical central germ.
- **claim-compactification** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-compactification)): Let $C$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic curve with marked point $0$. Let $g_c(x_1,x_2,x_3)$ be polynomials of bounded degree $l$, with coefficients regular on $C$, satisfying $g_c(0)=0$ and $d_xg_c(0)=0$. Suppose the critical point at the origin is isolated and has the same finite Milnor number $\mu$ for every $c\in C$. After replacing $C$ by a Zariski neighborhood of $0$, there is a flat projective hypersurface family $X\subset\mathbb P^3\times C$ of some fixed degree $d$, with section $\Gamma$, such that each fibre is integral and normal, smooth outside $\Gamma(c)$, and its germ at $\Gamma(c)$ admits a defining function analytically right-equivalent to $g_c$. Furthermore, $$\begin{aligned} K_{X_c}^2&=d(d-4)^2,\label{eq:compact-canonical-square}\\ \chi_c(X_c\setminus\{\Gamma(c)\}) &=d(d^2-4d+6)-\mu-1.\label{eq:compact-euler} \end{aligned}$$ The total space $X$ is normal and Gorenstein. These conclusions remain valid after a finite change of smooth base curves.
  - Explanation: A family of polynomials with constant Milnor number is compactified to a flat projective family of surfaces of one fixed degree with a single singular point on the section, with explicit canonical square and punctured Euler characteristic.
- **claim-log-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-log-model)): Let $C$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic curve with a marked point $0$, and let $\pi:X\to C$ be a flat projective family of integral normal Gorenstein surfaces. Suppose that $X$ is normal and Gorenstein, that a section $\Gamma\subset X$ contains every fibre singularity, and that $\pi$ is smooth on $X\setminus\Gamma$. Then, after shrinking $C$ about $0$ and making a finite base change from a smooth curve, there is a projective birational morphism $$h:Y\longrightarrow X$$ with the following properties. Here $X$, $C$, $\Gamma$ and $0$ denote the base-changed objects, and $H$ is the sum, with coefficient one, of all $h$-exceptional prime divisors dominating $C$.* 1. *$Y$ is normal and $\mathbb Q$-factorial, and $h$ is an isomorphism over $X\setminus\Gamma$.* 2. *For every $c\in C$, the scheme fibre $Y_c$ is a reduced Cartier divisor, and $(Y,H+Y_c)$ is divisorial log terminal.* 3. *$K_Y+H$ is nef over $X$. Every vertical $h$-exceptional prime divisor lies in $Y_0$ and maps to the point $\Gamma\cap X_0$.* 4. *For $c\ne0$, the surface $Y_c$ is integral and normal, and the divisor $H\|_{Y_c}$ is reduced as a Weil divisor. The fibre $Y_0$ has a unique component dominating $X_0$; its remaining components are projective surfaces over $\Gamma\cap X_0$.
  - Explanation: After a finite base change, the threefold relative minimal model program produces a birational model with a reduced Cartier special fiber forming a divisorial log terminal pair with the horizontal exceptional boundary, with log canonical class nef over the original family.
- **claim-surface-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-surface-boundary)): Every component $S$ of $Y_c$ is normal. Its different is exactly $B_S$: $$(K_Y+H+Y_c)\|_S\sim_{\mathbb Q}K_S+B_S.$$ In particular $(S,B_S)$ is dlt and $$L\|_S\sim_{\mathbb Q}K_S+B_S.$$ The latter divisor is nef on every curve contracted by $h\|_S$. For an extra vertical component $S_a$, it is nef on the projective surface $S_a$.
  - Explanation: Every fiber component is normal and adjunction gives as different exactly the reduced boundary, so each component pair is dlt and the restricted log canonical class is relatively nef.
- **claim-full-exceptional-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-full-exceptional-boundary)): Assume in addition that the surface $X_c$ is not log canonical at $\Gamma\cap X_c$ for general $c\in C$. Then $A\le0$, some horizontal coefficient of $A$ is strictly negative, and $$h^{-1}(\Gamma)=\operatorname{Supp}(-A) =\operatorname{Supp}(H)\cup\bigcup_a S_a$$ as sets. In particular $B_{S_0}$ is the full reduced exceptional preimage of $\Gamma\cap X_0$ on $S_0$, and $B_{Y_c}$ has the same property on every $Y_c$ with $c\ne0$. Thus $$S_0\setminus\operatorname{Supp}(B_{S_0}) \simeq X_0\setminus\Gamma, \qquad Y_c\setminus\operatorname{Supp}(B_{Y_c}) \simeq X_c\setminus\Gamma\quad(c\ne0).$$
  - Explanation: When general fibers are not log canonical along the section, the negativity lemma forces the whole preimage of the section to be the support of the boundary, so the boundary complements are the punctured fibers.
- **claim-boundary-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-cover)): Let $Z$ be a normal complex algebraic threefold and let $B=\sum_{j=1}^n B_j$ be a reduced divisor with distinct prime components. Assume that $(Z,B)$ is dlt and that every $B_j$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. If $z$ lies on at least two of the $B_j$, then, after replacing $Z$ by a neighborhood $U$ of $z$, there is a finite surjective map $\rho:\widetilde U\to U$ from a normal variety such that:* 1. *$\rho$ is étale in codimension one and over $U\setminus B$, and every total pullback $\rho^*B_j$ is a reduced Cartier divisor;* 2. *$(\widetilde U,\rho^*B)$ is dlt and is simple normal crossing at every point of $\rho^{-1}(z)$.
  - Explanation: Near a point on at least two boundary components of a dlt threefold pair, a finite cover unramified in codimension one makes the boundary reduced Cartier and the pulled-back pair simple normal crossing over that point.
- **claim-boundary-nearby-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-nearby-zero)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:boundary-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:boundary-cover">14</a>, suppose that a regular function $f$ has reduced zero divisor $V=\sum_{j\in I}B_j$, where $I$ is a nonempty subset of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$. Put $H=\sum_{j\notin I}B_j$. At every point $z\in V$ lying on at least two components of $B$, $$\psi_f(\mathbf1_{Z\setminus H})(z)=0.$$
  - Explanation: The nearby Euler function of the complement of the horizontal boundary vanishes at every point lying on at least two boundary components.
- **claim-nearby-euler-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-nearby-euler-comparison)): Let $q:Y\to C$ be a projective flat morphism from a normal complex threefold to a smooth pointed curve $(C,0)$. Let $H$ be a reduced divisor with horizontal prime components. Assume that the Cartier fiber $Y_0=\sum S$ is reduced, that $(Y,Y_0+H)$ is dlt, and that its boundary components are $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Assume also that $Y_t\setminus H$ is smooth for all sufficiently small $t\ne0$. For each component $S$ of $Y_0$, let $B_S$ be the reduced union of its intersections with the other components of $Y_0+H$, and put $S^\circ=S\setminus B_S$. If each local smoothing of an isolated singularity in $S^\circ$ has Milnor fiber Euler characteristic at least one, then $$\label{eq:nearby-euler-comparison} \chi_c(Y_t\setminus H) =\sum_{S\subset Y_0}\chi_c(S^\circ)+\delta, \qquad \delta\ge0,$$ for all sufficiently small $t\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The Euler characteristic of a nearby open fiber equals the sum over special-fiber components of the Euler characteristics of their boundary complements plus a nonnegative correction from interior singular points.
- **claim-interior-correction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-interior-correction)): Let $q:(\mathcal V,x)\to(\mathbb C,0)$ be a flat holomorphic germ whose scheme central fiber $(S,x)$ is a normal surface with an isolated singularity and whose sufficiently small nonzero fibers are smooth. If $F_x$ is a sufficiently small Milnor fiber of $q$, then $$\chi(F_x)\geq 1.$$ At a smooth point of a reduced Cartier central surface in a normal threefold, the local nearby Euler characteristic is $1$.
  - Explanation: A smoothing of a normal isolated surface singularity has Milnor fiber Euler characteristic at least that of a smooth point, and at a smooth point of a reduced Cartier central surface the nearby Euler value equals one.
- **claim-surface-bmy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-surface-bmy)): Let $S$ be a normal projective surface over $\mathbb C$, and let $B$ be a reduced effective Weil divisor, possibly empty. Suppose that $(S,B)$ is log canonical and that the $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $K_S+B$ is nef. Set $S^\circ=S\setminus\operatorname{Supp}B$. Then $$0\leq (K_S+B)^2 \leq 3e_{\mathrm{orb}}(S,B) \leq 3\chi_c(S^\circ),$$ where $e_{\mathrm{orb}}$ denotes Langer’s orbifold Euler number.
  - Explanation: For a log canonical projective surface pair with nef log canonical divisor, its square is nonnegative and bounded by three times the orbifold Euler number, which is at most the compactly supported Euler characteristic of the complement of the boundary.
- **claim-square-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-square-specialization)): Let $q:Y\to C$ be a flat projective morphism to a smooth connected complex curve, with pure two-dimensional fibres. Let $L$ be a rational Cartier divisor on $Y$. If $[Y_0]=\sum_j m_j[S_j]$ is the cycle of a fibre, then for every $c\in C$ $$(L\|_{Y_c})^2=\sum_j m_j(L\|_{S_j})^2.$$ In particular, the multiplicities on the right are one when $Y_0$ is reduced.
  - Explanation: In a flat projective family with pure two-dimensional fibers, the square of a divisor restricted to any fiber equals the multiplicity-weighted sum of squares on the components of a given fiber.
- **claim-beta-from-log-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-beta-from-log-model)): Let $X\to C$, with section $\Gamma$, be the projective family of Lemma <a href="#lem:compactification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:compactification">9</a>. Let $h:Y\to X$ be a projective birational morphism from a normal integral threefold, isomorphic off $\Gamma$. Suppose $Y_0=S_0+\sum_a S_a$ is reduced Cartier, $S_0$ maps birationally to $X_0$, and the $S_a$ map to $\Gamma(0)$. Suppose that, after shrinking $C$, the other fibres $Y_c$ are normal and map birationally to $X_c$.* *Let $H$ be the reduced horizontal exceptional boundary and set $L=K_Y+H$, assumed rational Cartier and $h$-nef. For each component $S$ of $Y_0$, let $B_S$ be the reduced divisor of its intersections with the other components of $Y_0+H$. Assume that $(S,B_S)$ is log canonical and $$L\|_S\sim_{\mathbb Q} K_S+B_S.$$ For $S_0$, require that $B_{S_0}$ is the full reduced inverse image of $\Gamma(0)$. For $c\ne0$, require likewise that the full reduced inverse image $B_c$ of $\Gamma(c)$ is a divisor, that $(Y_c,B_c)$ is log canonical, and that $L\|_{Y_c}\sim_{\mathbb Q}K_{Y_c}+B_c$. If $$(K_{S_a}+B_{S_a})^2=0\qquad\text{for every }a,$$ then $\beta(X_c,\Gamma(c))=\beta(X_0,\Gamma(0))$ for all remaining $c\in C$.
  - Explanation: If the log models satisfy the full-boundary, adjunction and nefness requirements and every extra component has vanishing logarithmic square, Wahl's invariant on the other fibers equals its central value.
- **claim-beta-constant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-beta-constant)): Let $F(x,t)$ be a holomorphic family of isolated hypersurface singularities in $\mathbb C^3$ with $f_t(0)=0$ and constant positive Milnor number. If the central normal surface germ $X_0=\{f_0=0\}$ is not log canonical, then $\beta(X_t,0)=\beta(X_0,0)$ for every sufficiently small $t$.
  - Explanation: In a constant-Milnor-number family with non-log-canonical central germ, Wahl's invariant is constant for small parameters. The Euler comparison, the interior sign and the surface inequality force every extra logarithmic square to vanish.
- **claim-resolved-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-resolved-family)): Let $F(x,t)=f_t(x)$ satisfy the hypotheses of the main theorem. Assume that $X_0=\{f_0=0\}$ is not log canonical and that $\beta(X_t,0)$ is constant. After shrinking the parameter disk and making a finite base change $t=u^e$, there is a proper modification $$b:N\longrightarrow X'=\{F(x,u^e)=0\}$$ such that $N\to\Delta_u$ is smooth, $b$ is an isomorphism off the origin section, and its reduced exceptional divisor $E$ has relative normal crossings. The restriction $b_u$ is the minimal semigood resolution, and $E\|_{N_u}$ is its full reduced exceptional divisor.
  - Explanation: With non-log-canonical central germ and constant invariant, after a finite base change there is a smooth family resolution, isomorphic off the origin section, with relative normal crossings exceptional divisor. Constancy of Euler characteristic rules out smoothing of nodes.
- **claim-ambient-tube** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ambient-tube)): Let $G(x,u)$ be holomorphic on a neighborhood of $B\times D$, where $B\subset\mathbb C^n$ is a closed ball centered at $0$ and $D\subset\mathbb C$ is a disk centered at $0$. Suppose that $G(0,u)=0$ and that $d_xG(x,u)\ne0$ for $x\ne0$. Assume that, for some $\eta>0$, the levels $G(\cdot,u)=c$, $\|c\|<\eta$, meet $\partial B$ transversely. Put $$T_u=\{x\in B:\|G(x,u)\|<\eta\},\qquad Z^*=\{(x,u)\in(B\setminus\{0\})\times D:G(x,u)=0\}.$$ Suppose there are smooth real vector fields $v_1,v_i$ on $Z^*$, tangent to its sphere boundary, projecting to $1,i$ on $D$. Suppose moreover that their transport along each segment $[0,u]$ and its reverse exists for the entire segment in $Z^*$. More precisely, this is the transport of $\operatorname{Re}(u)v_1+\operatorname{Im}(u)v_i$ for time one, starting over $0$, and its reverse starting over $u$. Then, after shrinking $D$ if necessary, there are homeomorphisms $$H_u:T_0\longrightarrow T_u, \qquad G(H_u(x),u)=G(x,0),$$ such that $H_0=\mathrm{id}$, $H_u(0)=0$, and both the family and its inverse are jointly continuous. Restriction to the interiors of the tubes is a parameter-preserving ambient homeomorphism near the origin section.
  - Explanation: Complete transport of parameter directions on the punctured zero family extends to ambient homeomorphisms of closed-ball tubes preserving the function. These maps and their inverses are jointly continuous at the origin section.
- **claim-whitney-right** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-whitney-right)): Let $G(x,u)$, with $x\in\mathbb C^n$, be a holomorphic family with $G(0,u)=0$ and no spatial critical points off the origin section near $(0,0)$. If the pair $$\bigl(\{G=0\}\setminus\{x=0\},\ \{x=0\}\bigr)$$ satisfies Whitney conditions <span class="upright">(a)</span> and <span class="upright">(b)</span>, then $G$ is ambiently topologically right trivial near that section, with the central map equal to the identity.
  - Explanation: If the punctured zero locus and the origin section satisfy Whitney conditions (a) and (b), the radius function is a submersion and the family is ambiently topologically right-trivial with identity central map.
- **claim-resolution-right** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-resolution-right)): Let $G(x,u)$ satisfy the analytic and transversality hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:ambient-tube" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ambient-tube">23</a>. Suppose that the zero family admits a proper holomorphic map $b:M\to\{G=0\}$ over the parameter disk such that* 1. *$q:M\to D$ is a smooth family;* 2. *$b$ is an isomorphism away from the origin section;* 3. *$E=b^{-1}(\{x=0\})$ is a relative normal-crossing divisor.* *Here the last condition means that, in local coordinates consisting of fiber coordinates and $u$, the support of $E$ is a union of coordinate hyperplanes in the fiber coordinates. Then $G$ has the trivialization of Lemma <a href="#lem:ambient-tube" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ambient-tube">23</a>.
  - Explanation: A proper resolution of the zero family that is smooth over the parameter disk with relative normal crossings exceptional divisor supplies complete lifts, hence the tube trivialization.
- **claim-remove-base-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-remove-base-change)): Let $F(x,t)$ be a holomorphic family with $F(0,t)=0$. Suppose that, after the base change $t=u^e$, $e\ge1$, the family $G(x,u)=F(x,u^e)$ has the tube trivialization $H_u$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:ambient-tube" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ambient-tube">23</a>. Then $F$ is ambiently topologically right trivial over the original disk, by a section-preserving homeomorphism whose central map is the identity.
  - Explanation: A tube trivialization after the cyclic base change is corrected along the angular seam to give a trivialization over the original disk with identity central map.
- **claim-low-cases** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-low-cases)): Let $F(x,t)=f_t(x)$ be a holomorphic family in three spatial variables, with $f_t(0)=0$, an isolated critical point at $0$ in every fiber, and constant Milnor number. If either $\operatorname{mult}_0(f_0)=2$ or the normal surface germ $X_0=\{f_0=0\}$ is log canonical, then $F$ is topologically right-trivial over a sufficiently small parameter disk.
  - Explanation: If the central multiplicity is two, or the central surface germ is log canonical, the family is topologically right-trivial. The proof splits off a square or uses sectional Milnor numbers with Teissier's criterion and Whitney regularity.
- **claim-supported-square** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-supported-square)): Let $q:Y\to C$ be a dominant morphism from an integral normal $\mathbb Q$-factorial quasi-projective complex threefold to a smooth connected complex algebraic curve. Let $A=\sum_i a_iE_i$ be a rational divisor whose support is proper over $C$. For a fiber write its fundamental cycle as $[Y_c]=\sum_jm_j[S_j]$. Then the number $$Q_c(A)=\sum_jm_j(A\|_{S_j})^2$$ is independent of $c$.* *Here, when $S_j$ is proper, its summand is the usual projective intersection number. When $S_j$ is not proper, it is not a component of $A$, and $A\|_{S_j}$ is a rational Cartier divisor with proper curve support. Its square means the degree obtained by intersecting that proper curve cycle with the rational line bundle $A\|_{S_j}$.
  - Explanation: The fiberwise sum of multiplicity-weighted squares of a divisor with support proper over the curve does not depend on the fiber.
- **claim-local-exceptional-square** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-local-exceptional-square)): Suppose a projective birational morphism $h:Y\to X$ is a log model over a smooth curve as in Section <a href="#sec:full-boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:full-boundary">5.1</a>, except that $X\to C$ need not be proper. Assume $X$ and $Y$ are quasi-projective, all exceptional prime divisors map into the section $\Gamma\simeq C$, and put $$A=K_Y+H-h^*K_X.$$ If $Y_0=S_0+\sum_aS_a$ and the nearby fibers are irreducible, then $$(A\|_{Y_c})^2=(A\|_{S_0})^2+\sum_a(A\|_{S_a})^2.$$ Under the full-boundary, adjunction, and nefness hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:beta-from-log-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:beta-from-log-model">20</a>, the first two squares are respectively $-\beta(X_c,\Gamma(c))$ and $-\beta(X_0,\Gamma(0))$, while $$(A\|_{S_a})^2=(K_{S_a}+B_{S_a})^2.$$ Thus vanishing of the extra squares proves constancy of the local invariant without a projective compactification of $X$.
  - Explanation: Without a projective compactification of the base family, the squares of the exceptional divisor on nearby and central fibers are minus the local invariant, so vanishing of the extra squares gives constancy.
- **claim-marked-monomial-reduction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-marked-monomial-reduction)): Let $q:Z\to C$ be a morphism from a smooth quasi-projective complex threefold to a smooth curve. Let $0\in C$, write $Z_0=\sum_i a_iZ_i$, and let $H$ be a reduced divisor with horizontal components. Suppose that $(Z_0)_{\mathrm{red}}+H$ is simple normal crossing, and that away from $0$ the morphism is smooth with relative simple normal crossing divisor $H$. After shrinking $C$ about $0$, choose a parameter $\tau$ with no other zero and an integer $b$ divisible by every $a_i$. After the finite base change $s^b=\tau$ and normalization, there is a projective birational modification whose total space is smooth and whose special fiber is reduced and simple normal crossing together with the transformed horizontal marking. The modification is an isomorphism on every open set where the original morphism was smooth and the marking relatively simple normal crossing.
  - Explanation: After a cyclic base change and normalization, compatible star subdivisions of toric charts give a smooth projective modification with reduced simple-normal-crossing special fiber together with the transformed horizontal marking.
- **claim-jet-component-comparison** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-jet-component-comparison)): Let $U_\mu$ be the locally closed algebraic jet locus of Lemma <a href="#lem:mu-jet-locus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:mu-jet-locus">7</a>. Assume the algebraic constancy assertion in Proposition <a href="#prop:scalar-curve-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scalar-curve-reduction">8</a>. Any two points of the same irreducible component of $U_\mu$, one of which defines a non-log-canonical surface germ, have the same Wahl invariant. Consequently that proposition holds without invoking constructibility of the Wahl invariant.
  - Explanation: Granting the algebraic constancy assertion, any two points of one irreducible component of the jet locus, one non-log-canonical, share the Wahl invariant. This avoids constructibility of the invariant.
- **claim-continuous-gradient-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-continuous-gradient-lift)): Assume that $d_xG\ne0$ off $A$ and that, at every point of $A$, $$\label{eq:gradient-small-o} \frac{\|G_u(x,u)\|}{\\|d_xG(x,u)\\|}\longrightarrow0 \qquad\text{as }(x,u)\longrightarrow A,\quad x\ne0.$$ For each $a=1,i$, suppose that $X^*$ carries a smooth real vector field $v_a=(w_a,a)$ with the following properties:* 1. *$dG(v_a)=0$ and $w_a(x,u)\to0$ on approach to every point of $A$;* 2. *no trajectory of $v_a$ in $X^*$ has a finite-time endpoint in $A$ lying inside the representative, in either time direction.* *Then, after shrinking, there is an ambient homeomorphism germ $$H(x,u)=(H_u(x),u),\qquad G(H_u(x),u)=G(x,0),\qquad H_u(0)=0,$$ with $H_0=\mathrm{id}$ and with $H$ and $H^{-1}$ jointly continuous.
  - Explanation: A small-order gradient estimate and lifted fields with vanishing spatial part and no finite-time arrival at the section produce an ambient homeomorphism germ trivializing the function, with jointly continuous inverse.
- **claim-gradient-whitney** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-gradient-whitney)): Suppose that $G$ satisfies the analytic and gradient hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:continuous-gradient-lift" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:continuous-gradient-lift">33</a> and that $(X^*,A)$ satisfies the Whitney conditions. Then $G$ has the trivialization of that proposition. The fields on $X^*$ can be chosen to preserve $\\|x\\|^2$, and every constant real combination has the same property.
  - Explanation: Under the gradient hypotheses and Whitney conditions, the lifted fields can be chosen to preserve the squared radius, so the continuous-lift criterion applies.
- **claim-gradient-resolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-gradient-resolution)): Suppose that $G$ satisfies the analytic and gradient hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:continuous-gradient-lift" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:continuous-gradient-lift">33</a>. Suppose that there is a proper holomorphic map $b:M\to\{G=0\}$ over $D$, an isomorphism away from $A$, such that the composite $q:M\to D$ is smooth and $E=b^{-1}(A)$ is a relative normal-crossing divisor. Then $G$ has the trivialization of that proposition. The fields on $X^*$ satisfy the trajectory condition for every constant real combination.
  - Explanation: Under the gradient hypotheses and a proper resolution that is smooth over the disk with relative normal crossings exceptional divisor, lifts exist that satisfy the trajectory condition for every constant real combination.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-projective-local-square",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be an integral normal projective complex surface, smooth outside a point $p$, with Cartier canonical divisor. Let $b:S\\to V$ be a projective birational morphism from a normal surface, isomorphic outside $p$. Suppose that the reduced inverse image of $p$ is a divisor $B$, that $(S,B)$ is log canonical, and that $K_S+B$ is $b$-nef. Then $$\\label{eq:projective-local-square}\n \\beta(V,p)=K_V^2-(K_S+B)^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compactification",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic curve with marked point $0$. Let $g_c(x_1,x_2,x_3)$ be polynomials of bounded degree $l$, with coefficients regular on $C$, satisfying $g_c(0)=0$ and $d_xg_c(0)=0$. Suppose the critical point at the origin is isolated and has the same finite Milnor number $\\mu$ for every $c\\in C$. After replacing $C$ by a Zariski neighborhood of $0$, there is a flat projective hypersurface family $X\\subset\\mathbb P^3\\times C$ of some fixed degree $d$, with section $\\Gamma$, such that each fibre is integral and normal, smooth outside $\\Gamma(c)$, and its germ at $\\Gamma(c)$ admits a defining function analytically right-equivalent to $g_c$. Furthermore, $$\\begin{aligned}\n K_{X_c}^2&=d(d-4)^2,\\label{eq:compact-canonical-square}\\\\\n \\chi_c(X_c\\setminus\\{\\Gamma(c)\\})\n   &=d(d^2-4d+6)-\\mu-1.\\label{eq:compact-euler}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The total space $X$ is normal and Gorenstein. These conclusions remain valid after a finite change of smooth base curves.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compactification",

Furthermore, $$\\begin{aligned}\n K_{X_c}^2&=d(d-4)^2,\\label{eq:compact-canonical-square}\\\\\n \\chi_c(X_c\\setminus\\{\\Gamma(c)\\})\n   &=d(d^2-4d+6)-\\mu-1.\\label{eq:compact-euler}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The total space $X$ is normal and Gorenstein.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-log-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic curve with a marked point $0$, and let $\\pi:X\\to C$ be a flat projective family of integral normal Gorenstein surfaces. Suppose that $X$ is normal and Gorenstein, that a section $\\Gamma\\subset X$ contains every fibre singularity, and that $\\pi$ is smooth on $X\\setminus\\Gamma$. Then, after shrinking $C$ about $0$ and making a finite base change from a smooth curve, there is a projective birational morphism $$h:Y\\longrightarrow X$$ with the following properties. Here $X$, $C$, $\\Gamma$ and $0$ denote the base-changed objects, and $H$ is the sum, with coefficient one, of all $h$-exceptional prime divisors dominating $C$.*\n\n1.  *$Y$ is normal and $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, and $h$ is an isomorphism over $X\\setminus\\Gamma$.*\n\n2.  *For every $c\\in C$, the scheme fibre $Y_c$ is a reduced Cartier divisor, and $(Y,H+Y_c)$ is divisorial log terminal.*\n\n3.  *$K_Y+H$ is nef over $X$. Every vertical $h$-exceptional prime divisor lies in $Y_0$ and maps to the point $\\Gamma\\cap X_0$.*\n\n4.  *For $c\\ne0$, the surface $Y_c$ is integral and normal, and the divisor $H|_{Y_c}$ is reduced as a Weil divisor. The fibre $Y_0$ has a unique component dominating $X_0$; its remaining components are projective surfaces over $\\Gamma\\cap X_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-log-model",

Then, after shrinking $C$ about $0$ and making a finite base change from a smooth curve, there is a projective birational morphism $$h:Y\\longrightarrow X$$ with the following properties. Here $X$, $C$, $\\Gamma$ and $0$ denote the base-changed objects, and $H$ is the sum, with coefficient one, of all $h$-exceptional prime divisors dominating $C$.*\n\n1.  *$Y$ is normal and $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, and $h$ is an isomorphism over $X\\setminus\\Gamma$.*\n\n2.  *For every $c\\in C$, the scheme fibre $Y_c$ is a reduced Cartier divisor, and $(Y,H+Y_c)$ is divisorial log terminal.*\n\n3.  *$K_Y+H$ is nef over $X$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-full-exceptional-boundary",
        "exact_quote": "Assume in addition that the surface $X_c$ is not log canonical at $\\Gamma\\cap X_c$ for general $c\\in C$. Then $A\\le0$, some horizontal coefficient of $A$ is strictly negative, and $$h^{-1}(\\Gamma)=\\operatorname{Supp}(-A)\n              =\\operatorname{Supp}(H)\\cup\\bigcup_a S_a$$ as sets. In particular $B_{S_0}$ is the full reduced exceptional preimage of $\\Gamma\\cap X_0$ on $S_0$, and $B_{Y_c}$ has the same property on every $Y_c$ with $c\\ne0$. Thus $$S_0\\setminus\\operatorname{Supp}(B_{S_0})\n       \\simeq X_0\\setminus\\Gamma,\n \\qquad\n Y_c\\setminus\\operatorname{Supp}(B_{Y_c})\n       \\simeq X_c\\setminus\\Gamma\\quad(c\\ne0).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-064.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a normal complex algebraic threefold and let $B=\\sum_{j=1}^n B_j$ be a reduced divisor with distinct prime components. Assume that $(Z,B)$ is dlt and that every $B_j$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. If $z$ lies on at least two of the $B_j$, then, after replacing $Z$ by a neighborhood $U$ of $z$, there is a finite surjective map $\\rho:\\widetilde U\\to U$ from a normal variety such that:*\n\n1.  *$\\rho$ is étale in codimension one and over $U\\setminus B$, and every total pullback $\\rho^*B_j$ is a reduced Cartier divisor;*\n\n2.  *$(\\widetilde U,\\rho^*B)$ is dlt and is simple normal crossing at every point of $\\rho^{-1}(z)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-nearby-euler-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a projective flat morphism from a normal complex threefold to a smooth pointed curve $(C,0)$. Let $H$ be a reduced divisor with horizontal prime components. Assume that the Cartier fiber $Y_0=\\sum S$ is reduced, that $(Y,Y_0+H)$ is dlt, and that its boundary components are $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Assume also that $Y_t\\setminus H$ is smooth for all sufficiently small $t\\ne0$. For each component $S$ of $Y_0$, let $B_S$ be the reduced union of its intersections with the other components of $Y_0+H$, and put $S^\\circ=S\\setminus B_S$. If each local smoothing of an isolated singularity in $S^\\circ$ has Milnor fiber Euler characteristic at least one, then $$\\label{eq:nearby-euler-comparison}\n \\chi_c(Y_t\\setminus H)\n   =\\sum_{S\\subset Y_0}\\chi_c(S^\\circ)+\\delta,\n \\qquad \\delta\\ge0,$$ for all sufficiently small $t\\ne0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-nearby-euler-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a projective flat morphism from a normal complex threefold to a smooth pointed curve $(C,0)$. Let $H$ be a reduced divisor with horizontal prime components. Assume that the Cartier fiber $Y_0=\\sum S$ is reduced, that $(Y,Y_0+H)$ is dlt, and that its boundary components are $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Assume also that $Y_t\\setminus H$ is smooth for all sufficiently small $t\\ne0$. For each component $S$ of $Y_0$, let $B_S$ be the reduced union of its intersections with the other components of $Y_0+H$, and put $S^\\circ=S\\setminus B_S$. If each local smoothing of an isolated singularity in $S^\\circ$ has Milnor fiber Euler characteristic at least one, then $$\\label{eq:nearby-euler-comparison}\n \\chi_c(Y_t\\setminus H)\n   =\\sum_{S\\subset Y_0}\\chi_c(S^\\circ)+\\delta,\n \\qquad \\delta\\ge0,$$ for all sufficiently small $t\\ne0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-064.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a projective flat morphism from a normal complex threefold to a smooth pointed curve $(C,0)$. Let $H$ be a reduced divisor with horizontal prime components. Assume also that $Y_t\\setminus H$ is smooth for all sufficiently small $t\\ne0$. For each component $S$ of $Y_0$, let $B_S$ be the reduced union of its intersections with the other components of $Y_0+H$, and put $S^\\circ=S\\setminus B_S$. If each local smoothing of an isolated singularity in $S^\\circ$ has Milnor fiber Euler characteristic at least one, then $$\\label{eq:nearby-euler-comparison}\n \\chi_c(Y_t\\setminus H)\n   =\\sum_{S\\subset Y_0}\\chi_c(S^\\circ)+\\delta,\n \\qquad \\delta\\ge0,$$ for all sufficiently small $t\\ne0$.",
      "explanation": "The Euler characteristic of a nearby open fiber equals the sum over special-fiber components of the Euler characteristics of their boundary complements plus a nonnegative correction from interior singular points.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $q:(\\mathcal V,x)\\to(\\mathbb C,0)$ be a flat holomorphic germ whose scheme central fiber $(S,x)$ is a normal surface with an isolated singularity and whose sufficiently small nonzero fibers are smooth. If $F_x$ is a sufficiently small Milnor fiber of $q$, then $$\\chi(F_x)\\geq 1.$$ At a smooth point of a reduced Cartier central surface in a normal threefold, the local nearby Euler characteristic is $1$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-surface-bmy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a normal projective surface over $\\mathbb C$, and let $B$ be a reduced effective Weil divisor, possibly empty. Suppose that $(S,B)$ is log canonical and that the $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $K_S+B$ is nef. Set $S^\\circ=S\\setminus\\operatorname{Supp}B$. Then $$0\\leq (K_S+B)^2\n    \\leq 3e_{\\mathrm{orb}}(S,B)\n    \\leq 3\\chi_c(S^\\circ),$$ where $e_{\\mathrm{orb}}$ denotes Langer’s orbifold Euler number.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-surface-bmy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a normal projective surface over $\\mathbb C$, and let $B$ be a reduced effective Weil divisor, possibly empty. Then $$0\\leq (K_S+B)^2\n    \\leq 3e_{\\mathrm{orb}}(S,B)\n    \\leq 3\\chi_c(S^\\circ),$$ where $e_{\\mathrm{orb}}$ denotes Langer’s orbifold Euler number.",
          "source_id": "PM-064.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $S$ be a normal projective surface over $\\mathbb C$, and let $B$ be a reduced effective Weil divisor, possibly empty. Suppose that $(S,B)$ is log canonical and that the $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $K_S+B$ is nef. Set $S^\\circ=S\\setminus\\operatorname{Supp}B$. Then $$0\\leq (K_S+B)^2\n    \\leq 3e_{\\mathrm{orb}}(S,B)\n    \\leq 3\\chi_c(S^\\circ),$$ where $e_{\\mathrm{orb}}$ denotes Langer’s orbifold Euler number.",
      "explanation": "For a log canonical projective surface pair with nef log canonical divisor, its square is nonnegative and bounded by three times the orbifold Euler number, which is at most the compactly supported Euler characteristic of the complement of the boundary.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-square-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a flat projective morphism to a smooth connected complex curve, with pure two-dimensional fibres. Let $L$ be a rational Cartier divisor on $Y$. If $[Y_0]=\\sum_j m_j[S_j]$ is the cycle of a fibre, then for every $c\\in C$ $$(L|_{Y_c})^2=\\sum_j m_j(L|_{S_j})^2.$$ In particular, the multiplicities on the right are one when $Y_0$ is reduced.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-square-specialization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a flat projective morphism to a smooth connected complex curve, with pure two-dimensional fibres. Let $L$ be a rational Cartier divisor on $Y$. If $[Y_0]=\\sum_j m_j[S_j]$ is the cycle of a fibre, then for every $c\\in C$ $$(L|_{Y_c})^2=\\sum_j m_j(L|_{S_j})^2.$$ In particular, the multiplicities on the right are one when $Y_0$ is reduced.",
          "source_id": "PM-064.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a flat projective morphism to a smooth connected complex curve, with pure two-dimensional fibres. Let $L$ be a rational Cartier divisor on $Y$. If $[Y_0]=\\sum_j m_j[S_j]$ is the cycle of a fibre, then for every $c\\in C$ $$(L|_{Y_c})^2=\\sum_j m_j(L|_{S_j})^2.$$ In particular, the multiplicities on the right are one when $Y_0$ is reduced.",
      "explanation": "In a flat projective family with pure two-dimensional fibers, the square of a divisor restricted to any fiber equals the multiplicity-weighted sum of squares on the components of a given fiber.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-beta-from-log-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X\\to C$, with section $\\Gamma$, be the projective family of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:compactification\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:compactification\">9</a>. Let $h:Y\\to X$ be a projective birational morphism from a normal integral threefold, isomorphic off $\\Gamma$. Suppose $Y_0=S_0+\\sum_a S_a$ is reduced Cartier, $S_0$ maps birationally to $X_0$, and the $S_a$ map to $\\Gamma(0)$. Suppose that, after shrinking $C$, the other fibres $Y_c$ are normal and map birationally to $X_c$.*\n\n*Let $H$ be the reduced horizontal exceptional boundary and set $L=K_Y+H$, assumed rational Cartier and $h$-nef. For each component $S$ of $Y_0$, let $B_S$ be the reduced divisor of its intersections with the other components of $Y_0+H$. Assume that $(S,B_S)$ is log canonical and $$L|_S\\sim_{\\mathbb Q} K_S+B_S.$$ For $S_0$, require that $B_{S_0}$ is the full reduced inverse image of $\\Gamma(0)$. For $c\\ne0$, require likewise that the full reduced inverse image $B_c$ of $\\Gamma(c)$ is a divisor, that $(Y_c,B_c)$ is log canonical, and that $L|_{Y_c}\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}K_{Y_c}+B_c$. If $$(K_{S_a}+B_{S_a})^2=0\\qquad\\text{for every }a,$$ then $\\beta(X_c,\\Gamma(c))=\\beta(X_0,\\Gamma(0))$ for all remaining $c\\in C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },

Suppose that, after shrinking $C$, the other fibres $Y_c$ are normal and map birationally to $X_c$.*\n\n*Let $H$ be the reduced horizontal exceptional boundary and set $L=K_Y+H$, assumed rational Cartier and $h$-nef. For each component $S$ of $Y_0$, let $B_S$ be the reduced divisor of its intersections with the other components of $Y_0+H$. Assume that $(S,B_S)$ is log canonical and $$L|_S\\sim_{\\mathbb Q} K_S+B_S.$$ For $S_0$, require that $B_{S_0}$ is the full reduced inverse image of $\\Gamma(0)$. For $c\\ne0$, require likewise that the full reduced inverse image $B_c$ of $\\Gamma(c)$ is a divisor, that $(Y_c,B_c)$ is log canonical, and that $L|_{Y_c}\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}K_{Y_c}+B_c$.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-beta-constant",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F(x,t)$ be a holomorphic family of isolated hypersurface singularities in $\\mathbb C^3$ with $f_t(0)=0$ and constant positive Milnor number.         "source_id": "PM-064.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $F(x,t)$ be a holomorphic family of isolated hypersurface singularities in $\\mathbb C^3$ with $f_t(0)=0$ and constant positive Milnor number.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-resolved-family",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F(x,t)=f_t(x)$ satisfy the hypotheses of the main theorem. Assume that $X_0=\\{f_0=0\\}$ is not log canonical and that $\\beta(X_t,0)$ is constant. After shrinking the parameter disk and making a finite base change $t=u^e$, there is a proper modification $$b:N\\longrightarrow X'=\\{F(x,u^e)=0\\}$$ such that $N\\to\\Delta_u$ is smooth, $b$ is an isomorphism off the origin section, and its reduced exceptional divisor $E$ has relative normal crossings. The restriction $b_u$ is the minimal semigood resolution, and $E|_{N_u}$ is its full reduced exceptional divisor.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ambient-tube",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G(x,u)$ be holomorphic on a neighborhood of $B\\times D$, where $B\\subset\\mathbb C^n$ is a closed ball centered at $0$ and $D\\subset\\mathbb C$ is a disk centered at $0$. Suppose that $G(0,u)=0$ and that $d_xG(x,u)\\ne0$ for $x\\ne0$. Assume that, for some $\\eta>0$, the levels $G(\\cdot,u)=c$, $|c|<\\eta$, meet $\\partial B$ transversely. Put $$T_u=\\{x\\in B:|G(x,u)|<\\eta\\},\\qquad\n Z^*=\\{(x,u)\\in(B\\setminus\\{0\\})\\times D:G(x,u)=0\\}.$$ Suppose there are smooth real vector fields $v_1,v_i$ on $Z^*$, tangent to its sphere boundary, projecting to $1,i$ on $D$. Suppose moreover that their transport along each segment $[0,u]$ and its reverse exists for the entire segment in $Z^*$. More precisely, this is the transport of $\\operatorname{Re}(u)v_1+\\operatorname{Im}(u)v_i$ for time one, starting over $0$, and its reverse starting over $u$. Then, after shrinking $D$ if necessary, there are homeomorphisms $$H_u:T_0\\longrightarrow T_u,\n \\qquad G(H_u(x),u)=G(x,0),$$ such that $H_0=\\mathrm{id}$, $H_u(0)=0$, and both the family and its inverse are jointly continuous. Restriction to the interiors of the tubes is a parameter-preserving ambient homeomorphism near the origin section.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },

Put $$T_u=\\{x\\in B:|G(x,u)|<\\eta\\},\\qquad\n Z^*=\\{(x,u)\\in(B\\setminus\\{0\\})\\times D:G(x,u)=0\\}.$$ Suppose there are smooth real vector fields $v_1,v_i$ on $Z^*$, tangent to its sphere boundary, projecting to $1,i$ on $D$.         "exact_quote": "Let $G(x,u)$, with $x\\in\\mathbb C^n$, be a holomorphic family with $G(0,u)=0$ and no spatial critical points off the origin section near $(0,0)$.         "exact_quote": "Let $G(x,u)$ satisfy the analytic and transversality hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:ambient-tube\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:ambient-tube\">23</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-supported-square",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a dominant morphism from an integral normal $\\mathbb Q$-factorial quasi-projective complex threefold to a smooth connected complex algebraic curve. Let $A=\\sum_i a_iE_i$ be a rational divisor whose support is proper over $C$. For a fiber write its fundamental cycle as $[Y_c]=\\sum_jm_j[S_j]$. Then the number $$Q_c(A)=\\sum_jm_j(A|_{S_j})^2$$ is independent of $c$.*\n\n*Here, when $S_j$ is proper, its summand is the usual projective intersection number. When $S_j$ is not proper, it is not a component of $A$, and $A|_{S_j}$ is a rational Cartier divisor with proper curve support. Its square means the degree obtained by intersecting that proper curve cycle with the rational line bundle $A|_{S_j}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-supported-square",
          "exact_quote": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a dominant morphism from an integral normal $\\mathbb Q$-factorial quasi-projective complex threefold to a smooth connected complex algebraic curve. Then the number $$Q_c(A)=\\sum_jm_j(A|_{S_j})^2$$ is independent of $c$.*\n\n*Here, when $S_j$ is proper, its summand is the usual projective intersection number. When $S_j$ is not proper, it is not a component of $A$, and $A|_{S_j}$ is a rational Cartier divisor with proper curve support. Its square means the degree obtained by intersecting that proper curve cycle with the rational line bundle $A|_{S_j}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-064.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $q:Y\\to C$ be a dominant morphism from an integral normal $\\mathbb Q$-factorial quasi-projective complex threefold to a smooth connected complex algebraic curve.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-local-exceptional-square",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a projective birational morphism $h:Y\\to X$ is a log model over a smooth curve as in Section <a href=\"#sec:full-boundary\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:full-boundary\">5.1</a>, except that $X\\to C$ need not be proper. Assume $X$ and $Y$ are quasi-projective, all exceptional prime divisors map into the section $\\Gamma\\simeq C$, and put $$A=K_Y+H-h^*K_X.$$ If $Y_0=S_0+\\sum_aS_a$ and the nearby fibers are irreducible, then $$(A|_{Y_c})^2=(A|_{S_0})^2+\\sum_a(A|_{S_a})^2.$$ Under the full-boundary, adjunction, and nefness hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:beta-from-log-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:beta-from-log-model\">20</a>, the first two squares are respectively $-\\beta(X_c,\\Gamma(c))$ and $-\\beta(X_0,\\Gamma(0))$, while $$(A|_{S_a})^2=(K_{S_a}+B_{S_a})^2.$$ Thus vanishing of the extra squares proves constancy of the local invariant without a projective compactification of $X$.",
        "source_id": "PM-064.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-local-exceptional-square",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a projective birational morphism $h:Y\\to X$ is a log model over a smooth curve as in Section <a href=\"#sec:full-boundary\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:full-boundary\">5.1</a>, except that $X\\to C$ need not be proper. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **topologically right-trivial family** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The family is *topologically right-trivial* if a homeomorphism germ of the ambient family, preserving the parameter and the origin section, identifies each defining function with the central one.
- **Wahl logarithmic invariant beta** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The invariant is $$\beta=-P_{\mathrm{loc}}^2.$$
- **good resolution** ([source](#sec:local-square)): Let $(V,p)$ be a normal complex surface singularity and let $\rho:\widetilde V\to V$ be a good resolution: $\widetilde V$ is smooth, $\rho$ is proper and isomorphic away from $p$, and the full reduced exceptional divisor $E=\sum_jE_j$ has simple normal crossings.
- **log canonical pair** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The pair is *log canonical*, abbreviated lc, if all log discrepancies are nonnegative.
- **divisorial log terminal pair** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A pair with boundary coefficients at most one is *divisorial log terminal*, abbreviated dlt, if it has a log resolution on which every exceptional divisor has positive log discrepancy.
- **Q-factorial variety** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A normal variety is $\mathbb Q$-factorial if every prime Weil divisor has a positive Cartier multiple.
- **nef over a morphism** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A divisor is nef over a morphism when it has nonnegative degree on every complete contracted curve.
- **nearby Euler function** ([source](#sec:nearby-euler)): If $f:Z\to\mathbb C$ is regular, the *nearby Euler function* $\psi_f$ is the unshifted nearby-cycle operation on constructible functions.
- **semigood resolution** ([source](#sec:resolution)): A surface resolution is *semigood* when its reduced exceptional divisor has normal crossings; irreducible components may have self-nodes.
- **relative normal crossings** ([source](#sec:resolution)): For a divisor in a smooth family of surfaces, *relative normal crossings* means that near its points there are coordinates $(z_1,z_2,u)$ in which the family map is $u$ and the divisor is $z_1=0$ or $z_1z_2=0$.
- **sectional Milnor numbers** ([source](#sec:low-cases)): For a holomorphic germ in $n$ variables with an isolated critical point, write $\mu^{(j)}$ for the Milnor number of its restriction to a general $j$-dimensional linear subspace, and put $\mu^*=(\mu^{(1)},\ldots,\mu^{(n)})$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-fixed-ball-euler | Euler characteristic of the zero fiber in the fixed ball | = | $1$ | All parameters in the smaller disk |
| q-punctured-euler | Compactly supported Euler characteristic of the punctured zero fiber in the ball | = | $0$ | All parameters in the smaller disk |
| q-jet-degree | Taylor degree sufficient for analytic right equivalence | ≥ | $\mu+1$ | Single germ with Milnor number mu |
| q-wahl-projective | Wahl local logarithmic invariant on a projective model | = | $K_V^2-(K_S+B)^2$ | Single point and nef log canonical partial resolution |
| q-canonical-square | Canonical square of the projective fiber | = | $d(d-4)^2$ | Fixed degree d, every fiber |
| q-compact-euler | Compactly supported Euler characteristic of the punctured projective fiber | = | $d(d^2-4d+6)-\mu-1$ | Fixed degree d, constant Milnor number mu |
| q-compact-degree | Degree of the compactifying hypersurfaces | ≥ | $\mu+2$ | Degree chosen above the bounded degree of the polynomials |
| q-bmy-extra | Logarithmic square of an extra component | ≤ | $3\chi_c(S_a^\circ)$ | Each extra component |
| q-interior-euler | Euler characteristic of the Milnor fiber of an interior smoothing | ≥ | $1$ | Sufficiently small Milnor fiber |
| q-delta-nonneg | Total interior smoothing excess | ≥ | $0$ | All small nonzero parameters |
| q-multiplicity-upper | Multiplicity of a log canonical surface germ | ≤ | $3$ | Central fiber |
| q-sectional-milnor | Sectional Milnor number sequence for multiplicity three | = | $(2,4,\mu(f_0))$ | Sufficiently small parameter disk |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-local-family** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-family-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-family-properties): Regular sequence and conservation of the spatial-gradient number, then Serre's criterion -> Critical locus is the section; total space and fibers are normal Gorenstein and flat
- **step-fixed-ball** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-ball-euler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-ball-euler): Milnor ball transversality and additivity of Euler characteristic with an inner ball -> Uniform ball with Euler characteristic of zero fiber one
- **step-finite-determinacy** [claim](#stmt-lem-individual-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-individual-jets): Flow of a time-dependent vector field using the Jacobian ideal and Nakayama -> Right equivalence to Taylor polynomials
- **step-local-square** [claim](#stmt-lem-projective-local-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projective-local-square): Log resolution, relative Zariski decomposition and projection formula -> Projective formula for the local invariant
- **step-lc-persistence** [claim](#stmt-lem-lc-persistence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lc-persistence): Spectrum constancy, threshold formula and inversion of adjunction -> Log canonicity constant in the family
- **step-jet-locus** [claim](#stmt-lem-mu-jet-locus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mu-jet-locus): Rank conditions, resolution and generic smoothness, Noetherian induction -> Locally closed jet locus with constructible invariant
- **step-curve-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar-curve-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scalar-curve-reduction): Curve selection by Noether normalization and contradiction with the algebraic assertion -> Constancy of the invariant in holomorphic families reduced to algebraic curves
- **step-compactification** [claim](#stmt-lem-compactification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compactification): Homogenize, add a general high-degree tail, use Bertini and adjunction -> Projective family with a single singular point and explicit invariants
- **step-monomial-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-marked-monomial-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-marked-monomial-reduction): Toric charts, star subdivisions and normalized blowups -> Smooth model with reduced special fiber
- **step-log-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-log-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-log-model): Log resolution, marked monomial reduction, relative minimal model program -> Dlt model with reduced Cartier fibers and nef log canonical class
- **step-surface-boundary** [claim](#stmt-lem-surface-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-surface-boundary): Dlt adjunction and local ring computation of the different -> Component pairs dlt with reduced boundary as different
- **step-full-boundary** [claim](#stmt-prop-full-exceptional-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-full-exceptional-boundary): Negativity lemma and support statement, with non-log-canonicity forcing a negative horizontal coefficient -> Preimage of the section is the support of the boundary
- **step-boundary-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-cover): Root cover making the boundary Cartier and discrepancy formula for finite covers -> Simple normal crossing cover over boundary intersection points
- **step-boundary-nearby-zero** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-nearby-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-nearby-zero): Euler characteristic of coordinate-product Milnor fibers and proper pushforward -> Vanishing nearby Euler function at boundary intersections
- **step-nearby-euler** [claim](#stmt-prop-nearby-euler-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nearby-euler-comparison): Integrate the nearby Euler function over the special fiber -> Euler comparison with nonnegative excess
- **step-interior-correction** [claim](#stmt-lem-interior-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interior-correction): Betti number vanishing of the first Betti number and Stein surface homotopy bound -> Milnor fiber Euler characteristic at least one
- **step-surface-bmy** [claim](#stmt-lem-surface-bmy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-surface-bmy): Orbifold Bogomolov-Miyaoka-Yau inequality and comparison of orbifold and ordinary Euler numbers -> Square bounded by three times the Euler characteristic of the complement
- **step-square-specialization** [claim](#stmt-lem-square-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-square-specialization): Constancy of Euler characteristics in flat families and comparison of leading Hilbert coefficients -> Fiberwise square equals weighted sum over components
- **step-beta-from-log-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-beta-from-log-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-beta-from-log-model): Specialize the square and apply the projective local square formula on both fibers -> Equality of local invariants given vanishing extra squares
- **step-beta-constant** [claim](#stmt-thm-beta-constant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-beta-constant): Euler cancellation forces every extra square to vanish, then specialize the square -> Wahl invariant constant for non-log-canonical constant-Milnor-number families
- **step-resolved-family** [claim](#stmt-prop-resolved-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-resolved-family): Simultaneous resolution theorem after base change, then Euler characteristic of exceptional curves excludes node smoothing -> Smooth resolved family with relative normal crossings exceptional divisor
- **step-ambient-tube** [claim](#stmt-lem-ambient-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ambient-tube): Extend lifted fields, integrate flows, and prove continuity at the section by compactness -> Tube homeomorphisms preserving the function
- **step-whitney-right** [claim](#stmt-cor-whitney-right) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-whitney-right): Whitney conditions make the radius a submersion, giving radius-preserving lifts -> Right triviality from Whitney regularity
- **step-resolution-right** [claim](#stmt-prop-resolution-right) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-resolution-right): Partition of unity on the resolution gives complete lifts tangent to the divisor, pushed down -> Tube trivialization from a resolution
- **step-remove-base-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-remove-base-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-remove-base-change): Angular seam correction by composing with the inverse of the transport between conjugate parameters -> Trivialization over the original disk
- **step-low-cases** [claim](#stmt-prop-low-cases) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-low-cases): Splitting off a square or sectional Milnor numbers with equimultiplicity, Teissier's criterion and Whitney regularity -> Right triviality for double points and log canonical germs
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-20): Case split between low cases and non-log-canonical case, then lift and descend -> Topological right triviality of the family
- **step-supported-square** [claim](#stmt-lem-supported-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-supported-square): Reduce to proper intersection numbers and constancy of degrees in flat families -> Invariance of the supported square
- **step-local-exceptional-square** [claim](#stmt-cor-local-exceptional-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-local-exceptional-square): Apply the supported square lemma and identify the squares through good resolutions -> Constancy criterion without compactification
- **step-jet-component** [claim](#stmt-lem-jet-component-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-jet-component-comparison): Join two points by a Bertini complete-intersection curve inside a jet component -> Equality of the invariant within a jet component
- **step-gradient-lift** [claim](#stmt-prop-continuous-gradient-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-continuous-gradient-lift): Corrected gradient lift, Peano existence and uniqueness, and composition of flows -> Continuous ambient trivialization
- **step-gradient-whitney** [claim](#stmt-cor-gradient-whitney) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-gradient-whitney): Radius-preserving projected lift using Whitney condition (b) -> Trivialization from Whitney regularity
- **step-gradient-resolution** [claim](#stmt-cor-gradient-resolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-gradient-resolution): Lifts on the resolution tangent to the divisor, pushed down, with the trajectory condition by properness -> Trivialization from a resolution

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-right-trivial-constant-mu**: When the Milnor number is constant along a holomorphic family of isolated hypersurface singularities in three variables, the family is topologically right-trivial after shrinking. The trivialization fixes the parameter and the origin section, preserves the defining function, and is jointly continuous together with its inverse.
- **inv-lc-persistence**: Along a holomorphic family of isolated hypersurface singularities in three spatial variables with constant Milnor number over a connected smooth complex curve, log canonicity of the surface germ does not change.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-infer-whitney**: Deduce Whitney regularity or smooth equisingularity of the zero set from constancy of the Milnor number alone. -> The result is only topological; topological triviality need not imply Whitney regularity, and the generic plane-section Milnor number may change.
- **anti-global-trivialization**: Apply the trivializing homeomorphism on a whole affine space or on a fixed large ball. -> The homeomorphism exists only on small neighborhoods of the origin section, so global use is unjustified.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is the existence of a homeomorphism germ obtained from flows of vector fields on resolved tubes, from resolution and minimal model theory, and from inequalities among Euler characteristics. No finite procedure with an explicit input and output is given, so there is nothing to execute and compare with an independent oracle.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper is a pure existence theorem on topological triviality of holomorphic families. It gives no finite procedure that a program could execute, so there is no construction to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns germs of complex hypersurfaces and homeomorphisms on small neighborhoods. It gives no physical or discrete dynamics to simulate. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem could in principle justify a certificate: constant Milnor number implies right-triviality. But the paper supplies no verified tool or procedure for this, so only a loose adaptation is conceivable. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Existence of the homeomorphism is proved through resolutions, the minimal model program and vector-field flows. No terminating computation, complexity bound or explicit output is stated. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure, index or representation scheme is introduced. The objects are analytic germs, divisors and Euler characteristics. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns arithmetic kernels, parallel execution or hardware mapping. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper works over the complex numbers with no hardness assumption, adversary model or security notion. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, loss or statistical model. The only conceivable link, topological data analysis of level sets, would need real data, whereas the hypotheses are strictly complex holomorphic. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimisation problem is posed. The inequalities in the paper are Euler characteristic and intersection bounds used inside a proof. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, consensus or fault model appears; the parameter is a single complex disk coordinate. |
| other | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The natural use is as a theoretical criterion in singularity theory and computer algebra: for a one-parameter holomorphic family in three variables with isolated critical points, constancy of the Milnor number implies topological right-triviality. This is a reasoning rule, not an algorithm, so it is only adaptable. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-isolated-critical-point** (hypothesis): The main theorem requires an isolated critical point at the origin for every parameter and constant Milnor number. Without isolatedness the Milnor number is not defined as a finite length. Blocked use: ['Applying the right-triviality conclusion to non-isolated singularities', 'Applying it to families whose Milnor number jumps at some parameter']
- **limit-local-shrinking** (formal_scope): The trivialization is a germ statement. It holds only after shrinking the disk and the germ representatives. Blocked use: ['Using the homeomorphism on a fixed large ball or on all of affine space', 'Assuming a uniform neighborhood size independent of the family']
- **limit-topological-only** (formal_scope): The conclusion is a homeomorphism with jointly continuous inverse, not a smooth or Whitney-regular equivalence. Topological triviality need not imply Whitney regularity. Blocked use: ['Inferring Whitney regularity or smooth equisingularity from constancy of the Milnor number alone', 'Using the maps as diffeomorphisms or with derivative control']
- **limit-three-variables-complex** (hypothesis): All hypotheses are over the complex numbers with exactly three spatial variables. The argument uses surface geometry and complex algebraic geometry. Blocked use: ['Transferring the result to real zero loci', 'Applying the proof to other numbers of variables']
- **limit-nonlc-branch-numerical** (hypothesis): Constancy of Wahl's invariant is proved only when the central surface germ is not log canonical. The log canonical and double-point cases are handled separately by Whitney regularity. Blocked use: ['Quoting constancy of the logarithmic invariant for log canonical central germs from this theorem']
- **limit-finite-base-change** (edge_case): The resolved family exists only after a finite disk base change t=u^e. Descent to the original disk uses a seam correction and requires the tube trivialization over the cover. Blocked use: ['Assuming the simultaneous resolution exists over the original disk', 'Assuming equivariance of lifts under deck transformations']
- **limit-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The proof uses existence theorems (minimal model program, simultaneous resolution, curve selection) and flows of vector fields. It gives no explicit homeomorphism or algorithm. Blocked use: ['Presenting the result as a procedure producing the trivializing map', 'Claiming any complexity bound']
- **limit-conditional-curve-reduction** (hypothesis): The reduction to algebraic curves is conditional on an algebraic assertion for polynomial families over curves, which the later sections prove. Blocked use: ['Using the reduction proposition without the algebraic assertion']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every holomorphic one-parameter family of isolated hypersurface singularities in ℂ<sup>3</sup> with constant Milnor number is topologically right-trivial. This gives a positive answer to the surface case of the <i>μ</i>-constant problem. The trivialization fixes the parameter and the origin section, and preserves the defining functions.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Milnor number measures the topology of a nearby smooth level of an isolated hypersurface singularity: its Milnor fiber has the homotopy type of a bouquet of spheres, with one sphere for each unit of the Milnor number (Milnor 1968, Theorems 6.5 and 7.2) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Milnor1968](#ref-Milnor1968)]; the analytic formulation in terms of the Jacobian algebra is recalled in (Lê and Ramanujam 1976, sec. 1, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[LeRamanujam1976](#ref-LeRamanujam1976)]. A basic question in equisingularity theory asks whether keeping this number constant also keeps the defining function topologically unchanged. For complex surfaces, this requires a method that works beyond the dimensions in which the classical cobordism argument applies.

Let $\Delta\subset\mathbb C$ be a disk centered at zero and let $$F:(\mathbb C^3\times\Delta,\{0\}\times\Delta)\longrightarrow(\mathbb C,0)$$ be holomorphic. Write $f_t(x)=F(x,t)$ and suppose that $f_t$ has an isolated critical point at the origin. Its Milnor number is $$\mu(f_t,0)=\dim_{\mathbb C}
 \frac{\mathcal O_{\mathbb C^3,0}}
 {(\partial f_t/\partial x_1,\partial f_t/\partial x_2,
   \partial f_t/\partial x_3)}.$$ The family is *topologically right-trivial* if a homeomorphism germ of the ambient family, preserving the parameter and the origin section, identifies each defining function with the central one. The coordinate changes and their inverses must vary jointly continuously with the parameter.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $F:(\mathbb C^3\times\Delta,\{0\}\times\Delta)\to(\mathbb C,0)$ be holomorphic. Suppose that, for every $t\in\Delta$, $f_t(0)=0$, $f_t$ has an isolated critical point at $0$, and $\mu(f_t,0)=\mu(f_0,0)$. After shrinking the disk and the germ representatives, there is a homeomorphism germ $$\Phi(x,t)=(\phi_t(x),t),\qquad
 \phi_t(0)=0,\quad \phi_0=\mathrm{id},\qquad
 f_t\bigl(\phi_t(x)\bigr)=f_0(x).$$ Both $\Phi$ and $\Phi^{-1}$ are jointly continuous on neighborhoods of the origin section over the original disk.*

</div>

Thus the surface case of the $\mu$-constant problem has a positive answer. The parameter dependence may be arbitrary and holomorphic, and the surface singularities need not be log canonical. The finite parameter covers used in the proof are removed before constructing the homeomorphism in the theorem. The same ambient maps identify the zero sets and the nearby nonzero levels, keeping the value of the function fixed throughout the parameter disk.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The problem and its antecedents

Lê and Ramanujam proved that a $\mu$-constant family of isolated hypersurface singularities has constant embedded topological type when the hypersurface dimension is different from two (Lê and Ramanujam 1976, Theorem 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LeRamanujam1976](#ref-LeRamanujam1976)]. Their argument compares Milnor fibers through a homologically trivial cobordism. In higher dimensions the $h$-cobordism theorem turns this comparison into a product; the curve case follows from surface topology. Complex dimension two is the dimension in which this argument leaves the product structure unresolved. Outside complex dimension two, Timourian and King established local triviality of families of function germs (Timourian 1977; King 1980) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Timourian1977](#ref-Timourian1977), [King1980](#ref-King1980)]. This additional passage matters here: constancy of the individual topological types and a jointly continuous choice of ambient coordinate changes are different conclusions.

Whitney regularity supplies a stronger geometric form of equisingularity. For a hypersurface germ in three variables, let $\mu^{(j)}$ be the Milnor number of its restriction to a general $j$-dimensional linear subspace. Then $\mu^{(3)}=\mu$, while $\mu^{(1)}$ is the multiplicity minus one and $\mu^{(2)}$ records the generic plane section. Teissier proved that constancy of this full sequence implies Whitney regularity along the singular section (Teissier 1973, chap. II, Theorem 3.9) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Teissier1973](#ref-Teissier1973)]; Briançon and Speder proved the converse (Briançon and Speder 1976, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BrianconSpeder1976](#ref-BrianconSpeder1976)]. Their examples also show that topological triviality need not imply Whitney regularity (Briançon and Speder 1975) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BrianconSpeder1975](#ref-BrianconSpeder1975)] <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BrianconSpeder1976](#ref-BrianconSpeder1976)](Briançon and Speder 1976, sec. 3). Thus a proof under the sole $\mu$-constant hypothesis must account for families whose generic plane-section Milnor number changes.

The equimultiplicity theorem of Fernández de Bobadilla and Pełka settles a separate part of this numerical comparison: a family with constant finite Milnor number has constant multiplicity in every dimension (Fernández de Bobadilla and Pełka 2024, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[FernandezBobadillaPelka2024](#ref-FernandezBobadillaPelka2024)]. Their theorem allows even formal power series whose coefficients vary continuously along a real interval. Its proof gives A’Campo’s radius-zero monodromy model a symplectic structure and extends McLean’s Floer-theoretic spectral sequence to compare multiplicities without assuming a common Milnor radius. For the present surface problem it supplies constancy of $\mu^{(1)}$, but does not supply constancy of $\mu^{(2)}$. We use it with Teissier’s criterion in the log-canonical case; the double-point case reduces to the classical $\mu$-constant problem for plane curves.

Vector fields offer another approach to the parameter dependence. The Lê–Saito–Teissier criterion relates constancy of the Milnor number to the smallness of the parameter derivative compared with the spatial gradient (Lê and Saito 1973) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[LeSaito1973](#ref-LeSaito1973)] <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Teissier1973](#ref-Teissier1973)](Teissier 1973, chap. II, Remark 3.10). Such an estimate makes a gradient lift continuous at the singular section, but continuity alone does not give unique, continuously invertible transport. Parusiński constructed trivializing vector fields for $\mu$-constant linear families $f(x)+tg(x)$ of isolated hypersurface singularities, without a dimension restriction (Parusiński 1999, Corollary 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Parusinski1999](#ref-Parusinski1999)]. Appendix <a href="#sec:continuous-gradient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:continuous-gradient">12</a> separates the gradient estimate from the trajectory control needed for general parameter dependence.

For surfaces, simultaneous resolution gives a bridge from numerical invariants to topology. Wahl associated a logarithmic characteristic number to a normal surface singularity (Wahl 1990) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Wahl1990](#ref-Wahl1990)]. Okuma showed that, for a deformation of a non-log-canonical Gorenstein surface singularity, constancy of this number produces simultaneous surface resolutions after finite base change, with reduced exceptional divisors on the fibers over the distinguished singular section (Okuma 2004, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Okuma2004](#ref-Okuma2004)].

Recent work describes further constraints on degenerations under the $\mu$-constant hypothesis. Aldasoro Rosales studies semistable models after suitable base change and projective compactification. Her results include rational acyclicity of the special-fiber dual complex, Tate type for the second cohomology of the additional vertical surfaces, and equality of the first Betti numbers of the special fiber and its distinguished component (Aldasoro Rosales 2025, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[AldasoroRosales2025](#ref-AldasoroRosales2025)]. The comparison below likewise studies these additional surfaces, using their boundary complements and logarithmic canonical squares. Peters proves symplectic equivalence of completed Milnor fibers and contact equivalence of links for $\mu$-constant polynomial families without vanishing folds (Peters 2026, Theorem 3.7) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Peters2026](#ref-Peters2026)]. That extra hypothesis provides a common Milnor radius. Our argument instead obtains transport from a resolved zero family and extends it to the ambient function levels.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The resolution invariant and the numerical comparison

Write $X_t=\{f_t=0\}$. These are normal Gorenstein surfaces. For non-log-canonical germs, our main numerical task is to deduce constancy of Wahl’s logarithmic surface invariant from $\mu$ alone. On a good resolution, write the relative Zariski decomposition of the canonical divisor plus the full reduced exceptional divisor as $P+N$, with $P$ relatively nef and $N$ effective and exceptional. The exceptional intersection matrix identifies a unique exceptional rational cycle $P_{\mathrm{loc}}$ having the same intersections as $P$ with every exceptional curve.
